## 2023-01-11 · Multiplex specific IgE and total IgE

Test

Specific IgE measures sensitization. Clinical allergy requires concordant history. The report gives <0.3 kUA/L as negative. The introduction, cross-reactivity education and repeated displays of reactive allergens are separate from analyte results.

Specimen collection · clinical laboratory report

## 2023-05 – 2023-09 · Confirmed infection and six-week recovery

Milestone

Positive SARS-CoV-2 test in May 2023 (likely third infection); symptoms for about 6 weeks, followed by intermittent feverish episodes that summer.

month range · patient report

Source: context_cards:CTX-003 · S3:4-6, S3:406-411

* Long COVID since Oct 2023 with severe neuro and gut symptoms
* 3rd COVID infection

May:

## 2023-05 – 2023-09 · Six-week infection followed by intermittent sick spells

Baseline

Patient recalls a positive test in May and symptoms lasting six weeks, then brief feverish or sick spells with intervals of feeling normal over summer.

retrospective months · Compared with pre-illness function · The summer symptoms are intermittent. Exact onset and offset days and functional capacity between spells are unknown.

Source: baseline_states:BL-01 · S3:406–411

S3:406–411 · 2023
May:
* COVID positive infection, most likely the 3rd infection […]

## 2023-09 – 2023-10 · Transient throat and breathing symptoms: suspected mold exposure and possible COVID reinfection

Milestone

Musty indoor air and faulty air conditioning coincided with throat and breathing symptoms, resolving after repairs. Suspected mold exposure. Possible COVID reinfection around October 2023: indeterminate (presumptive positive) PCR, followed by a reported negative PCR.

month · patient report

Source: context_cards:CTX-005 · S3:412-419, S1:230, S1:10830-10831, S1:20233-20236

S3:412-419 · Sept:
* moving into new flat in tropical/equatorial climate
* flat has high humidity and mouldy-smelling air → respiratory symptoms, feeling of suffocation, extreme discomfort in throat […]

S1:230 · regarding the timeline, i'm not actually sure if I caught covid in Sept after moving into the new flat as the PCR test came back as "presumptive positive" and discussion with the lab tech suggested it was either very low viral load or false positive, so result is indeterminate. it could well be that my May infection had already set me up for LC, given some weird summer episodes, and all it took was a stressful time (new job, move to new country etc) and mycotoxin exposure to trigger it full force. mycotoxins are potent immune suppressors after all.

S1:10830-10831 · it's still unclear to me if i had an actual covid infx in sept as i had a "presumptive positive" test (ie low but not enough viral load detected), but that could be a tiny infx or just remnants of viral persistence from the May infx
He recalled feverishness, sore throat, brain fog and a presumptive positive test during a five-day illness. He then tested negative and briefly resumed work and exercise.

S1:20233-20236 · it was in oct 2023
i only received a partial positive but they couldn't tell conclusively. i suspect i wasn't even infected and it was just remaining virus from my previous may infx, at which point i hadn't tested.
Hmm. mAbs are likely less effective […]

## 2023-10 – 2023-12 · Relapsing illness became persistent

Milestone

Feverishness, sore throat, cognitive slowing and weakness recurred about every 2 weeks. Third episode persisted with severe cognitive dysfunction, panic, fragmented sleep and paresthesias. Needed family assistance to return home by December.

month range · patient report

Source: context_cards:CTX-004 · S3:417-428, S3:438-444

S3:417-428 · Oct-Nov:
* on a Saturday, suddenly feverish, scratchy throat, brain fog, weakness
* partial positive COVID test → likely low viral load […]

S3:438-444 · Dec:
* obtained a cytokine panel during a Long COVID assessment → elevated cytokines showing low-level chronic inflammation […]

## 2023-10 – 2023-11 · Relapsing illness became persistently disabling

Baseline

After two short illnesses separated by returns to usual activity, a third spell persisted. Patient described hours of profound cognitive shutdown, trouble operating a phone or opening a door, panic and fragmented sleep. In later chat he recalled being intermittently bedbound, then improving.

retrospective months · Compared with intervening return to work and exercise · The patient uses the word unconscious. No observed loss-of-consciousness assessment is supplied. Timing of individual relapses is approximate.

Source: baseline_states:BL-02 · S1:2087–2087, S1:2098–2098, S1:2108–2108, S3:417–427

S1:2087–2087 · He recalled spending hours apparently unconscious in bed during the first month of illness, yet being able to walk between episodes. He also described panic attacks with a racing heart, sometimes waking him. Symptoms lessened over time while he was pacing and had introduced MCT oil.

S1:2098–2098 · Recalled that the severe early crashes stopped from the day after he started MCT oil.

S1:2108–2108 · Recalled no longer being intermittently bedbound over the same period.

S3:417–427 · Oct-Nov:
* on a Saturday, suddenly feverish, scratchy throat, brain fog, weakness
* partial positive COVID test → likely low viral load […]

## 2024-02 – 2024-05 · Worsening during a two-month treatment course and subsequent energy recovery

Baseline

Brain burning and tinnitus increased during maraviroc plus atorvastatin. In May, he reported improved fatigue/energy and a return to the pre-course level during a multi-ingredient regimen, with an early plateau.

retrospective months · Compared with pre-maraviroc symptoms · Medication and supplement changes co-occurred. The recovery comparison is with pre-course function, not with pre-illness health.

Source: baseline_states:BL-04 · S3:455–461, S3:472–474

S3:455–461 · Feb-Apr:
* by now, nicergoline lost effectiveness → eNOS uncoupling?
* test liver enzymes before Maraviroc start: […]

S3:472–474 · May:
* start liposomal long COVID protocol: vitamins A/B/C/D/E/K, quercetin, CBD, curcumin, cordyceps, ganoderma, shiitake, pectin, broccoli, antioxidants, inulin+probiotics
* immediate improvement in fatigue/energy levels almost immediately → regained pre-Maraviroc baseline, but improvement plateaud shortly thereafter

## 2024-04-17 · NC protocol: rapid partial recovery after maraviroc

Milestone

NC protocol plus inulin/probiotic: B complex, DEKA, vitamin C, curcumin, CBD, cordyceps, ganoderma, shiitake, pectin, broccoli and antioxidants. Rapid improvement in fatigue and energy to approximately pre-maraviroc baseline, then plateau.

2024 tracker date with retrospective patient clarification

Source: clinical_summary:liposomal-protocol · S5:2142, source_lines:PZ54-2026-11-23:8–10, S3:472-486, PZ57-COURSE-REVIEW

## 2024-06-02 – 2024-10-20 · Neurological/PENE variability leading up to NADH

Baseline

The clinician identified the pre-NADH crashes as neurological PENE or brain-burning episodes. Pain could limit movement or speech. The diary also contains generic lack-of-strength ratings. The distinctive deep muscle fatigue and physical PEM first appeared after NADH. June 22 records about two hours unable to move or speak, September 22 about two hours barely able to move. Some days he could work productively.

Tracker history through October 20. This window includes the revised first exposure day. · Compared with Earlier function in the same tracker · The tracker dates the observations. The reviewer clarifies the treatment dates. S6 clarifies phenotype. Retrospective reports supply additional symptoms. Magnesium poisoning was a patient hypothesis. Exact dose and mechanism remain uncertain. S3:symptom:013 separately describes later CNS shutdown without muscle fatigue.

Source: baseline_states:BL-S5-PRE-NADH · PZ-R14-TX:1–1, S1:2711–2711, S1:2714–2715, S5:313–313, S5:1659–1659, S6:1–6

S1:2711–2711 · He distinguished prior episodes, in which severe head burning made movement difficult but still possible with effort, from newer weakness or paralysis felt more directly in the muscles.

S1:2714–2715 · and this time i felt it also when my brain wasn't burning, unlike before, so it seems to be independent now, whereas before i only had paralysis like events when my brain was "on fire" and more an action paralysis, and this time proper muscle fatigue

S5:313–313 · Notes: Around 1, I took 10 mL Kender+ and possibly used BEMER before or afterward. A few hours later, around 4, I had terrible brain burning, dizziness and weakness. I hypothesized that THC had been synthesized from the hemp. This was the patient's hypothesis. The episode lasted two hours and I could not move or speak. A small amount of black stool in the morning and evening was attributed by the patient to alginite. Strong abdominal pain before the evening bowel movement resolved afterward. [V15]

S5:1659–1659 · Notes: Terrible brain burning in the early afternoon, then I could barely move for two hours. A very severe low point. [DJ15]

S6:1–6 · this one should be the source of truth for the nadh sequence. since the other data points are later retellings.
pre-nadh "crashes" were purely neurological, ie PENE, brain burns, etc.
post NADH eg the "magnesium posioning" denotes deep muscle fatigue (Valoszinusitheto magnezium mergezes. Tul sok NADH-t ittam elozo nap, 5 orara ra Mg es ez tortent. Amugy eleg jol aludtam.) […]

Q-NADH-PHENOTYPE · The clinician identified the pre-NADH crashes as neurological PENE or brain-burning episodes. Pain could limit movement or speech. The diary also contains generic lack-of-strength ratings. The distinctive deep muscle fatigue and physical PEM first appeared after NADH. The clinician identifies the October 23 “magnesium poisoning” entry as new deep muscle fatigue. The October 25 account describes progressive sleepiness and weakness, unusually good sleep, near-paralysis the next day, muscle cramps, and tingling without the usual pains. On November 2 the patient explicitly contrasted prior movement limitation during brain burning with fatigue now felt directly in the muscles, including when the brain burning was absent. S6 clarifies phenotype. Retrospective reports supply additional symptoms. Magnesium poisoning was a patient hypothesis. Exact dose and mechanism remain uncertain. S3:symptom:013 separately describes later CNS shutdown without muscle fatigue.

## 2024-07 – 2025-03 · Discordant Borrelia test results

Milestone

July 2024: strongly positive Borrelia IgG ELISA, positive IgG line blot, negative IgM and PCR. March 2025: reportedly negative specialist Western blot. Doxycycline for 2 months without reported benefit.

month range · original report and patient recollection

Source: context_cards:CTX-026 · S4-R12 p1, S4-R13 p1, S4-R14 p1, S3:239-241, S3:657

S3:239-241 · * was put on it for 2 months due to Lyme suspicion after positive IgG test
* no improvement at all, instead brain burns got more severe, was barely "in there" by the end of the cure
* doxycycline can slow lymphatic drainage → lymphatic issues may play a role in brain burns

S3:657 · * Lyme test: a Lyme specialist tested via Western blot → Borrelia negative

## 2024-10-09 · Blood draw

Investigation

Initially tolerated well with maximum pulse 112. Pulse later reached 125 during breakfast/other activity and a crash followed around 3. The blood draw was not isolated as its cause. The clinician identified the pre-NADH crashes as neurological PENE or brain-burning episodes. Pain could limit movement or speech. The diary also contains generic lack-of-strength ratings. The distinctive deep muscle fatigue and physical PEM first appeared after NADH.



Source: context:context:e4179017-54a5-5e52-83e2-433ee910b294 · diary_responses:S5-R-EA15-1

## 2024-10-09 · Blood draw

Response

Initially tolerated well with maximum pulse 112. Pulse later reached 125 during breakfast/other activity and a crash followed around 3. The blood draw was not isolated as its cause. The clinician identified the pre-NADH crashes as neurological PENE or brain-burning episodes. Pain could limit movement or speech. The diary also contains generic lack-of-strength ratings. The distinctive deep muscle fatigue and physical PEM first appeared after NADH.

mixed · dated diary account, see wording for timing · temporal association; unconfirmed · Report dated 2024-10-09

Source: response:S5-R-EA15-1 · S5:1883-1883, Q-NADH-PHENOTYPE

S5:1883-1883 · Notes: The day started well. I felt well during the blood draw and my maximum pulse was 112. Afterward, during breakfast and other activities, it was 125. A crash occurred around 3. [EA15]

Q-NADH-PHENOTYPE · The clinician identified the pre-NADH crashes as neurological PENE or brain-burning episodes. Pain could limit movement or speech. The diary also contains generic lack-of-strength ratings. The distinctive deep muscle fatigue and physical PEM first appeared after NADH. The clinician identifies the October 23 “magnesium poisoning” entry as new deep muscle fatigue. The October 25 account describes progressive sleepiness and weakness, unusually good sleep, near-paralysis the next day, muscle cramps, and tingling without the usual pains. On November 2 the patient explicitly contrasted prior movement limitation during brain burning with fatigue now felt directly in the muscles, including when the brain burning was absent. S6 clarifies phenotype. Retrospective reports supply additional symptoms. Magnesium poisoning was a patient hypothesis. Exact dose and mechanism remain uncertain. S3:symptom:013 separately describes later CNS shutdown without muscle fatigue.

## 2024-10-20 – 2024-10-30 · NADH-associated severe baseline decline and onset of physical and neurological PEM

Milestone

After NADH on 20–21 and 25–28 October 2024: new profound deep-muscle fatigue and physical PEM, severe crash on 23 October, unable to get out of bed on 29 October. Previously able to leave home intermittently; thereafter frequently bedbound, with persistent PEM and sensory intolerance. Partial recovery in November, but markedly lower baseline. Brain burning/PENE predated NADH; neurological PEM was new.

day range · Reviewer-corrected intake dates; tracker, patient and clinician accounts of symptoms

S1-M1617 · The patient described severe neurological symptoms despite working with a clinical team. Gut symptoms had partly improved from constant pain and one bowel movement per week. Energy had improved, deep sleep had increased from about 20 minutes to 45–90 minutes, and testosterone had risen from the low end of the range. Brain fog and POTS persisted. He described a nonliposomal experimental nanocarrier formulation and relayed the developers’ claim that it could increase absorption of substances taken alongside it. He took a slightly larger than intended dose of the NADH formulation, then his usual magnesium dose about five hours later. He recalled a roughly 12-hour steady-state interval for the formulation. He became progressively sleepy and weak, slept unusually well, and felt effectively paralysed the next day. He had muscle cramps and tingling, without his usual pains and other symptoms. He called this magnesium toxicity but also considered an acute ME-related change. Symptoms began clearing as he drank large amounts of water. The team was considering a formulation intended to target the blood–brain barrier. He was considering switching to off-label trials if their experiments failed and was avoiding concurrent drugs because of the proposed absorption interaction.

S1-M2711 · tbf i was paralyzed before when the brain burning was so painful i didn't think i could move, but if i forced myself i could, so i suspect it was my brain trying to protect myself. however the new thing now is that i feel this paralysis more directly in the muscles

S1-M2714 · oh and this time i felt it also when my brain wasn't burning, unlike before, so it seems to be independent now, whereas before i only had paralysis like events when my brain was "on fire" and more an action paralysis, and this time proper muscle fatigue

S1-M2480 · i am stuck in bed today, feeling weak and almost as if tranquilized. it may be progression of ME. 😢

i have probably been taking too much NADH and chatgpt says it can cause either fatigue directly or at worst oxidative stress without proper oxygen levels, which in me that's a given. if i'm lucky it's just temporary, but the added stress may have worsened my baseline.

S1-M2509 · by now, my muscle numbness has gone down a little and PENE struck. by numbness i mean that it took so much willpower to move my limbs, but once i did it i could move. not sure if that's fatigue but it definitely feels like it starts in the brain like you hypothesized

S3:symptom:013 · * Post Exertional Malaise (PEM):
* exertion causes sudden shutdown of executive function, both cognitive and physical
* 1-3x/day, lasting a few hours, but sometimes recurring for days
* but it’s never a continuous crash even then
* progression: top of forearms/hands tingling (rapidly fluctuating, slightly corrosive, acid-like unpleasant sensation) → feet/calves tingling → nape tingling/pulsing → occipital lobe fatigue, pressures or sometimes headache → tranquilizing sensation and pulsing along spine → brain slows down suddenly (extreme CNS fatigue) → rarely almost full paralysis but usually just an intense malaise sensation (a bit like the flu), also tingling sometimes intensifies to a corrosion like pain → often narcoleptic sleep pressure, often fall asleep regardless of time of day
* not muscle fatigue!
* I can still move muscles, PEM seems to be in CNS with peripheral tingling as above, but if it’s very strong my “willpower” to move is zero
* unless in severe crash, eating immediately lifts all symptoms (including sleep pressure), sometimes as soon as the first bite
* Herxheimer reaction crashes are particularly violent, tingling is far more painful/corrosive
* “evening PEM”: every day starting 5-6pm, getting at least initial parts of above progression (ie at least tingling) → IL-1beta circadian upregulation?

## 2024-10-23 – 2024-10-29 · New post-NADH deep muscle fatigue and October decline

Baseline

NADH was taken October 20–21 and restarted October 25–28, 2024, according to the reviewer. New deep muscle fatigue and a major crash were first documented October 23. Marked weakness and confinement to bed recurred October 29. The patient attributed a major baseline drop to NADH. November records describe partial recovery followed by a later relapse. Magnesium and exertion were also discussed. The clinician identifies the October 23 “magnesium poisoning” entry as new deep muscle fatigue. The October 25 account describes progressive sleepiness and weakness, unusually good sleep, near-paralysis the next day, muscle cramps, and tingling without the usual pains. On November 2 the patient explicitly contrasted prior movement limitation during brain burning with fatigue now felt directly in the muscles, including when the brain burning was absent. Daily overall ratings were 5/10 on October 21–22, 2/10 on October 23, and 3–4/10 through October 28. On October 29 he woke with numb or weak arms, especially around the shoulders and hands, and needed about an hour to get up. The clinician identifies this as the new deep muscle-fatigue phenotype. Later that day he felt tranquilized and confined to bed. Moving his limbs took considerable effort but remained possible. Muscle symptoms then eased and PENE followed. The diary also records suspected overexertion the previous evening.

## 2024-10-23 – 2024-11-12 · New deep muscle fatigue and physical crashes after NADH

Episode

The clinician identifies the October 23 “magnesium poisoning” entry as new deep muscle fatigue. The October 25 account describes progressive sleepiness and weakness, unusually good sleep, near-paralysis the next day, muscle cramps, and tingling without the usual pains. On November 2 the patient explicitly contrasted prior movement limitation during brain burning with fatigue now felt directly in the muscles, including when the brain burning was absent. On October 29 he woke with numb or weak arms, especially around the shoulders and hands, and needed about an hour to get up. The clinician identifies this as the new deep muscle-fatigue phenotype. Later that day he felt tranquilized and confined to bed. Moving his limbs took considerable effort but remained possible. Muscle symptoms then eased and PENE followed. The diary also records suspected overexertion the previous evening.

The S5 calendar diary dates NADH initiation to the evening of October 21, 2024, excess NADH to October 22, and the first documented physical crash to October 23. Magnesium followed NADH by about five hours. The exact onset across the night of October 22–23 is unresolved. By November 9/12 he was no longer bedridden. Recovery and later relapse are separately documented.

S1-M1617 · The patient described severe neurological symptoms despite working with a clinical team. Gut symptoms had partly improved from constant pain and one bowel movement per week. Energy had improved, deep sleep had increased from about 20 minutes to 45–90 minutes, and testosterone had risen from the low end of the range. Brain fog and POTS persisted. He described a nonliposomal experimental nanocarrier formulation and relayed the developers’ claim that it could increase absorption of substances taken alongside it. He took a slightly larger than intended dose of the NADH formulation, then his usual magnesium dose about five hours later. He recalled a roughly 12-hour steady-state interval for the formulation. He became progressively sleepy and weak, slept unusually well, and felt effectively paralysed the next day. He had muscle cramps and tingling, without his usual pains and other symptoms. He called this magnesium toxicity but also considered an acute ME-related change. Symptoms began clearing as he drank large amounts of water. The team was considering a formulation intended to target the blood–brain barrier. He was considering switching to off-label trials if their experiments failed and was avoiding concurrent drugs because of the proposed absorption interaction.

S1-M2711 · tbf i was paralyzed before when the brain burning was so painful i didn't think i could move, but if i forced myself i could, so i suspect it was my brain trying to protect myself. however the new thing now is that i feel this paralysis more directly in the muscles

S1-M2714 · oh and this time i felt it also when my brain wasn't burning, unlike before, so it seems to be independent now, whereas before i only had paralysis like events when my brain was "on fire" and more an action paralysis, and this time proper muscle fatigue

S1-M2480 · i am stuck in bed today, feeling weak and almost as if tranquilized. it may be progression of ME. 😢

i have probably been taking too much NADH and chatgpt says it can cause either fatigue directly or at worst oxidative stress without proper oxygen levels, which in me that's a given. if i'm lucky it's just temporary, but the added stress may have worsened my baseline.

S1-M2509 · by now, my muscle numbness has gone down a little and PENE struck. by numbness i mean that it took so much willpower to move my limbs, but once i did it i could move. not sure if that's fatigue but it definitely feels like it starts in the brain like you hypothesized

S3:symptom:013 · * Post Exertional Malaise (PEM):
* exertion causes sudden shutdown of executive function, both cognitive and physical
* 1-3x/day, lasting a few hours, but sometimes recurring for days
* but it’s never a continuous crash even then
* progression: top of forearms/hands tingling (rapidly fluctuating, slightly corrosive, acid-like unpleasant sensation) → feet/calves tingling → nape tingling/pulsing → occipital lobe fatigue, pressures or sometimes headache → tranquilizing sensation and pulsing along spine → brain slows down suddenly (extreme CNS fatigue) → rarely almost full paralysis but usually just an intense malaise sensation (a bit like the flu), also tingling sometimes intensifies to a corrosion like pain → often narcoleptic sleep pressure, often fall asleep regardless of time of day
* not muscle fatigue!
* I can still move muscles, PEM seems to be in CNS with peripheral tingling as above, but if it’s very strong my “willpower” to move is zero
* unless in severe crash, eating immediately lifts all symptoms (including sleep pressure), sometimes as soon as the first bite
* Herxheimer reaction crashes are particularly violent, tingling is far more painful/corrosive
* “evening PEM”: every day starting 5-6pm, getting at least initial parts of above progression (ie at least tingling) → IL-1beta circadian upregulation?

S1-M3753 · today is worse. had a surprisingly okay day yesterday. think i spent too much time at my computer and have PENE today. not bedridden, so not as bad as a week back, but very brainfogged still.

S1-M4081 · i'm doing better, no longer bedridden. i think taking the basic vitamins has helped recovery from my crash, suggesting your redox imbalance theory may be correct. i saw rapid baseline improvement after starting supplementing them (same day, these nano-carriers hit very fast).

## 2024-10-25 – 2024-10-30 · Renewed deep muscle fatigue and subsequent PENE

Baseline

On October 29 he woke with numb or weak arms, especially around the shoulders and hands, and needed about an hour to get up. The clinician identifies this as the new deep muscle-fatigue phenotype. Later that day he felt tranquilized and confined to bed. Moving his limbs took considerable effort but remained possible. Muscle symptoms then eased and PENE followed. The diary also records suspected overexertion the previous evening. NADH was reported restarted the night of October 25 and stopped by October 30.

Restart October 25; final dose October 28 per reviewer. Crash October 29; reported stopped by October 30. · Compared with previous ability to leave the bed and use the toilet · The tracker dates the observations. The reviewer clarifies the treatment dates. S6 clarifies phenotype. Retrospective reports supply additional symptoms. Magnesium poisoning was a patient hypothesis. Exact dose and mechanism remain uncertain. S3:symptom:013 separately describes later CNS shutdown without muscle fatigue. The restart date is inferred from the October 26 message.

## 2024-11-09 – 2024-12-07 · No longer bedridden; intermittent near-baseline mobility

Baseline

By November 9 he was no longer bedridden, although still severely fogged. On November 12 he reiterated the mobility improvement and perceived benefit after resuming vitamins. On January 8 he recalled roughly two weeks bedridden, two weeks back at baseline without PEM, then another decline. A November 15 hospital trip provoked several hours of symptoms. On December 4 he felt roughly at his own baseline, and on December 7 could walk without dizziness while brain burning had subsided.

observations on Nov 9/12/15 and Dec 4/7; interval not continuously sampled · Compared with late October bedbound state · The December comparator is his own baseline at the time. Persistent brain fog and exertional flares complicate any claim of complete recovery or permanent loss from NADH alone.

## 2024-12-08 – 2024-12-29 · Very low threshold for visual and phone activity

Baseline

On December 8 he woke with mild PEM, then had a one-to-two-hour evening crash. By December 17 an hour of motionless rest could be followed by another crash after five minutes on a phone, with neck, arm and forehead sensations and weakness. He clarified that the paralysis-like sensation could allow movement with great effort. On December 21 he sat in the living room for several morning hours, then crashed. At month end he described relief in darkness without visual stimulation.

Intervention

The review estimates about one month, with an uncertain reference point. A February 2 hold and a March 1 dose change appear in the record. By March 29 the patient described a KPV-free hospital break and restarted. That later course has an April 6 stop. The evidence does not support assigning a final dose one month after either December 27 or March 29.

documented course

## 2025-01-03 – 2025-01-12 · Selected activities became possible during better windows

Baseline

He tolerated some classical music but described worsened burning or fog with much other music. He was frequently bedridden and too weak for consistent notes. He later recalled being able to leave the dark room for the first half of the day after January 8, with PEM and burning still recurring.

dated observations plus retrospective Jan 8 treatment history · Compared with winter dark-room state · An individual response could vary by day, intervention or activity. The duration of the improvement is uncertain.

patient reported result · Specific waveforms, latencies and original report are unavailable.

## 2025-03-25 – 2025-04 · Neurology admission and post-discharge deterioration

Milestone

mixed precision · patient report

## 2025-03-25 – 2025-03-28 · Neurology admission altered activity and treatment exposure

Baseline

In hospital the patient used an eye mask and wheelchair transport, stopped supplements and reported less walking. Even after resting, continuous scrolling could precipitate symptoms within 20–30 minutes. Home again on March 28.

dated admission and return home · Compared with usual home routine · Activity, stimulation, supplements and environment all changed. Performance in hospital and delayed symptoms after discharge are different observations.

Source: baseline_states:BL-15 · S1:29649–29649, S1:29809–29809, S1:30009–30009, S1:30035–30035, S1:30044–30044, S1:30084–30084

S1:30084–30084 · He reported walking much less during the admission because he was transported to tests by wheelchair.

## 2025-03-28 – 2025-05-03 · Post-discharge deterioration with fluctuating relief

Baseline

By March 31 he reported instability. By April 2 he described nearly continuous poisoned/tingling PEM. Some relief occurred April 4, but by April 7 he compared the new level to near-severe. In May he continued to relate lost progress to the admission period.

early deterioration directly dated; lost progress still attributed to admission May 3 · Compared with patient-described moderate pre-admission level · Boundaries denote reports of the change. Multiple simultaneous treatment interruptions and rest/activity changes preclude a single attributed cause or uniform severity across all days.

## 2025-04-08 · Ivabradine: reduction in resting and standing heart rate

Milestone

Ivabradine 5 mg twice daily for orthostatic tachycardia: reported heart-rate reduction of about 10 bpm at rest and 15–20 bpm standing; no clear broader clinical improvement. Temporarily paused in April, restarted 23 April.

Patient-reported treatment response · approximately dated start

## 2025-05-19 – 2025-06-08 · Variable cognitive tolerance and limited daily walking

Baseline

A poisoned crash cluster around May 19 overlapped with a new mixture and exertion. He later described a day of drawing without the usual PEM. A house move exceeded his activity budget; on June 8 he estimated 500–1,000 steps daily and said short walks could be tolerated.

May mixture dates disputed by a day; June 8 explicit report · Compared with post-hospital state · This is a mobility estimate for June, not a permanent daily step count. The drawing report describes a single better activity window.

## 2025-06-21 – 2025-06-30 · Repeated profound fatigue and sleep-pressure attacks

Baseline

Following one reported rapamycin dose, very intense afternoon crashes recurred over about ten days, including severe CNS/upper-body fatigue, corrosive tingling, inability to speak during worse attacks and renewed head burning/pressures. On June 26 he reported extreme sleepiness on four of five days.

dosing and indexed diary observations; recalled duration approximate · Compared with earlier fluctuating pattern with better mornings · This spans intermittent attacks, not uninterrupted immobility. Tea/other changes, background PEM and variable activity overlapped; recovery of the pre-trial baseline is not demonstrated here.

Source: baseline_states:BL-18 · S1:42316–42316, S1:42812–42812, S1:43399–43411, S2:246–260, S3:665–668

S1:42316–42316 · On June 22 he recalled sudden intense upper abdominal pain, lasting about a minute, a few hours after starting rapamycin and before the previous day’s crash. He distinguished the location from his usual right-sided flares.

S1:42812–42812 · He reported extreme sleepiness, which he called narcolepsy, on four of the first five days after starting rapamycin.

S1:43399–43411 · 1 tingling in arms legs begins
2 amplifies to corrosive pain
3 meanwhile overall energy/motivation drops, increasingly sensitive to light and sounds (same screen i was just looking st causes shivers down the spine)
4 not part of the symptom presentation but it is at this point i usually go from propped up on bed to curling up in fetal pose to rest motionless as it's the only bearable "activity" at this stage, eyes closed, sometimes still listening to audiobook though (on very loud volume)
5 if i'm lucky it slowly gets milder. otherwise, the poisoned feeling spreads to spine and brain, brain fatigue increases, and sensory sensitivity rises to extremes - brain pem/pene
6 optional, this really only started happening since rapa: brain burns and strong frontal lobe (helmet) as well as sinus pressures come on roughly at same time as brain pem
7 by now of course cognitive function crawls to a halt, i'm barely able to think, speaking feels impossible, or really, doing anything at this point is extremely hard because my brain is in so much pain and and is so sluggish that it feels like transmitting commands goes at snails pace.
8 during the worst crashes, after the above symptoms get better, i suddenly become very sleepy and usually fall asleep. it almost feels like my body became so exhausted from the above it now needs to go into recovery asap.
The patient added that sudden anger or frustration could briefly enable action despite difficulty initiating movement. Examples included standing to urinate after an hour, briefly raising the voice, and adjusting curtains in response to painful light or noise. […]

## 2025-07 – 2025-09 · Green stools, GI symptoms and dietary response

Milestone

Green stools after starting ensitrelvir on 1 July 2025; recurrent green diarrhea, upper abdominal pain and systemic tingling during Gou teng/SuperHMO trials and dietary changes. Transient stool-color improvement with modified citrus pectin, Enterosgel, activated charcoal, KPV and fat restriction; later abdominal pain largely resolved on a very low-fat diet. Patient also suspected benefit from PEA/luteolin; individual effects uncertain.

month range · patient report

## 2025-07-08 · Telmisartan; potassium

Response

Took telmisartan July 8, doubled dose by July 11, held with potassium July 12. No sleep improvement by July 15. Appetite changes during overlapping potassium exposure; anxiety recurred without telmisartan. The sleep/headache improvement elsewhere in the chat concerned someone else.

## 2025-07-15 – 2025-07-31 · Recurrent systemic flares and reduced speech tolerance

Baseline

Tingling and green diarrhea recurred during overlapping gou teng, SuperHMO and other exposures. He worried about a new fatigue phenotype. July 27 he could communicate using brief yes/no questions but had difficulty speaking at length and tracking conversation. July 31 a violent crash followed both recent activity and xylitol intake.

observed across July messages and corroborating diary; intermittent · Compared with prior communication and GI function · His prediction of a future baseline loss on July 20 is not evidence that a permanent loss occurred. Hearing protection and cognitive load also affected conversation.

S1:51440–51440 · He reported a severe crash after pancakes containing xylitol that morning. He also reported greater activity over the preceding day and regarded either exposure or both as possible contributors.

## 2025-10-24 · MicroGenDX urine qPCR and NGS

Test

Laboratory-developed DNA-based assay. Organism percentages are relative within bacterial/fungal results. Predicted susceptibilities come from a reference database, not measured phenotypic susceptibility. A resistance gene is not linked to an individual organism by this report. Receipt 2025-10-16; cover also prints acceptance 10.08.2025. Collection date not established. The image-based table reports a threshold for organisms. Its drug grid gives reference-based predictions rather than measured susceptibility.

Report date only · molecular laboratory developed test

Source: reports:S4-R24 · S4-R24

## 2025-10-24 · MicroGenDX nasal swab qPCR and NGS

Test

Laboratory-developed DNA-based assay. Organism percentages are relative within bacterial/fungal results. Susceptibilities are database predictions. The same fungal results repeat on p3. Receipt date is not a verified collection date. Image-based fungal results repeat on another page. The drug grid gives reference-based predictions rather than measured susceptibility.

Report date only · molecular laboratory developed test

## 2025-11-04 – 2025-11-21 · More laptop tolerance with dimming, red filter and pacing

Baseline

Despite occasional crashes he described being at his usual baseline on November 4. By November 21 he reported managing a dimmed/red laptop through the day by pacing passive media and some chatting. He contrasted this with immediate PEM from turning over in bed at the earlier winter nadir.

Nov 4 day and Nov 21 description of usual functioning · Compared with preceding winter most severely impaired state · Adaptive equipment and task selection changed what he could tolerate. This does not establish comparable capacity for work, ordinary screens or unpaced activity.

Source: baseline_states:BL-21 · S1:59821–59821, S1:61729–61731

S1:59821–59821 · i'm dong ok (at baseline) now. woke at 1.30 pm (slept at 6am, sadly due to yesterday's crash I reversed my circadian cycle again sigh)

S1:61729–61731 · He described better laptop tolerance than the previous winter, when even turning in bed could provoke severe symptoms. Marked screen dimming, a red filter and pacing allowed considerably longer use.
1/ installing software on laptop that drastically dims brightness and adds a red tinted overlay →suddenly was able to use laptop all day without crashing unlike before when the blue brightness crashed me almost immediately
2/ learning what activities i can do to keep dopamine constantly engaged but not push myself over energy budget (so mostly passive consumption sadly and some chatting)

## 2025-12-01 – 2025-12-19 · New persistent nape/wrist and forehead symptoms with a lower threshold

Baseline

Fatigue and intermittent head symptoms preceded the bezisterim dose. Marked CNS fatigue followed the next day amid concurrent probiotic and meals. By December 5 nape symptoms were nearly continuous. On December 16 standing to go to the toilet provoked symptoms even outside a perceived crash. By December 19 he was emerging from the deepest crash but felt below his former level.

diary dates and contemporaneous reports · Compared with pre-trial screen and activity tolerance · The patient explicitly questioned whether bezisterim, the spore probiotic, or an overlap explained the change. Subsequent state is a different question from causality.

Source: baseline_states:BL-22 · S1:62405–62405, S1:62477–62477, S1:64140–64140, S1:64699–64699, S2:3543–3569

At times severe poisoned malaise progressed to fatigue and sleep, after which he felt back at baseline. He could have a more functional start to the day and still sleep-crash later; animated conversation or intense typing/research brought on nape pain and fatigue.

## 2026-01-30 – 2026-02-15 · Marked fall in tolerance for speech, posture and typing

Baseline

During a cluster around SAG/glutathione and post-sexual flares he reported being mostly motionless in bed. Typing a phone message, sitting up or speaking a few words could immediately bring forearm fatigue, burning, nape sensations and forehead pressure. He could sometimes scroll or listen to a podcast.

onset of severe cluster approximate; functional comparison explicit Feb 14–15 · Compared with previous ability to type slowly or watch videos for hours · Prior capacity and early onset are recalled; objective strength or a specific causal exposure was not measured. Some grip symptoms were already becoming milder.

Sitting up, typing a message or speaking a few words could bring on immediate symptoms, beginning with burning or fatigue around the wrists and forearms and progressing to forehead or retro-orbital pressure. ## 2026-03-08 – 2026-03-26 · Severe Redaxin-period crash followed by reported recovery of the earlier baseline

Baseline

Following one relatively better day he described a severe crash with suicidal distress around the initial Redaxin trial. March 18 he thought he had recovered the level preceding the SAG/post-sexual cluster. By March 26 he described his usual daytime tingling, evening PEM and post-sexual poisoned crashes.

dated severe episode then two dated recovery comparisons · Compared with late January/February crash cluster · His comparator is the baseline before the most recent worsening. March improvement does not establish that the pre-bezisterim or pre-illness level returned.

## 2026-04-03 – 2026-05-16 · Reduced cognitive reserve and further patient-reported loss around KL1333

Baseline

April 3 he contrasted his limited current chatting with prolonged chatting months earlier. After small KL trials in April, he described greater reactivity and a lower usual level on May 7 and May 16 despite stopping. May 6 he reported continuous head symptoms and tingling even in a dark room at rest.

direct comparative reports Apr 3 and May 7/16; KL dosing from Apr 19 · Compared with pre-bezisterim chat tolerance; subsequent pre-KL baseline · Vehicle, dose, exertion and post-sexual symptoms varied. The sequence and persistence are documented; independent attribution and exact end date are unknown.

## 2026-06 – 2026-11 · Hepatitis C antivirals repurposed for suspected chronic COVID: Ledifos, Velpanat, Darvoni

Milestone

Ledifos, Velpanat and Darvoni trialed for suspected chronic COVID. Severe crash with brain discomfort and sleep after initial Velpanat and after a double Darvoni dose. Single-dose Darvoni reportedly tolerated for about 2 months, followed by tolerated Velpanat after switching back.

## 2026-06-06 · Estimated end 2026-06-06 to 2026-06-07 · Lithium orotate

Intervention

The June 6 diary records 10 mg lithium orotate at 02:30 and a mild post-intervention flare at 03:15. The retrospective history describes a milder nape flare within 45 minutes of lithium (S3:272–273). The timing matches, and the patient qualified the association. June 7 nape clenching is separately recorded. The reviewer recalls a one- or two-day trial and identifies Nutricost Lithium Orotate. June 6 is the only verified dose.

## 2026-07-08 – 2026-07-25 · Recurrent internal torso tremor interfered with sleep

Baseline

The patient described evening chest/solar-plexus hammering after an intense episode and subsequent stress; as little as about three hours sleep on some nights. Overexertion intensified the symptom. On July 25 he reported roughly one music track of the referenced length before burning and fatigue.

onset week approximate; chat and July 13 clock anchor · Compared with tremors previously infrequent outside severe crashes · Track length and general auditory ceiling were not measured. Sleep and tremor intensity fluctuated, including nights he could sleep despite the tremor.

## Event date unknown · NADH

Response

The patient associated NADH with a new deep-muscle fatigue and a major decline in function. Courses were recorded on 20–21 and 25–28 October 2024. Cognitive crashes had occurred before NADH.

attributed persistent worsening · undated retrospective history · patient retrospective · Patient-reported

Source: response:S3:treatment_response:209 · S3:209-211, Q-S5-NADH-START, * NADH
* developed PEM from it, had a massive crash only after 3 days of supplementing (never had such a crash before)
* likely due to upset NAD+/NADH balance and uncovering existing complex I dysfunction

S3:209-211 · * NADH
* developed PEM from it, had a massive crash only after 3 days of supplementing (never had such a crash before)
* likely due to upset NAD+/NADH balance and uncovering existing complex I dysfunction

Q-S5-NADH-START · The reviewer specifies October 20–21 and October 25–28, 2024, inclusive. The tracker records an evening start October 21 and recalls a dose October 22. The retrospective history gives later dates. These accounts disagree on exposure timing. Previous reconstruction: The S5 calendar diary dates NADH initiation to the evening of October 21, 2024, excess NADH to October 22, and the first documented physical crash to October 23. Magnesium followed NADH by about five hours. The exact onset across the night of October 22–23 is unresolved. S6 clarifies phenotype. Retrospective reports supply additional symptoms. Magnesium poisoning was a patient hypothesis. Exact dose and mechanism remain uncertain. S3:symptom:013 separately describes later CNS shutdown without muscle fatigue. S5 does not name a restart. S1-M1899 supports an inferred October 25 night restart. The later S3 account places it October 28–29. An October 23 all-lipo hold does not specify NADH.

> 15.50- pem/malaise/brain fatigue intensifying. think i’ll crash soon. it’s a sudden state change, everything is significantly worse, as if some dial was sharply adjusted. tingling is now slower and more burn-y, bit like lactate. brain slowed down, also burny. everything feels more fragile too, as in, i can feel that any sudden movement or effort would be punished more harshly, or that my brain and body would fall apart. brainstem and nape area more tender, pulsing with mild pain from the inside. sinus sensations and mild pressures more tender also. or perhaps i’m more sensitized and feel them stronger. some fatigue in wrists and triceps right now; besides the cns.
